### `sharegpt_hRCFRol_0`

- Facts: **90** · Keyphrases: **12** · Events: **7**

#### Facts

1. *(turn 0)* I requested a reduction of the power loss of the previous test 96_20 by using a reduced oil level of -0.5 lt.
2. *(turn 0)* I tested the 755/10145957 MF Front Agri axle with reduced oil level (-0.5 lt.) using oil ELF SF3.
3. *(turn 0)* I performed a P&T test measuring power loss versus rpm up to 3234 rpm (53 kph) at 90°C and 60°C with ambient temperature 25°C for FWD and REV.
4. *(turn 0)* I performed a P&T test at 53 kph FWD to measure power loss and temperature raise.
5. *(turn 0)* I performed a lube test with different combinations of speed, direction, and temperatures, including 0° monitoring oil levels in critical areas.
6. *(turn 0)* I used reference tests 02_17 (755/611 M2), 05_17 (740/623 M2-19), and 96_20 (755 M2-21 std oil level).
7. *(turn 0)* The test was completed without any issue, with no lubrication issues and no oil leak.
8. *(turn 0)* In the main results for test 96_20, the power loss versus rpm at 90°C was 3.60 kW (4.55 kW in parentheses).
9. *(turn 0)* In the main results for test 96_20, the power loss versus rpm at 60°C was 4.2 kW (5.7 kW in parentheses).
10. *(turn 0)* In the main results for test 96_20, the power loss versus rpm at ambient temperature was 8.1 kW (10.3 kW in parentheses) after run-in.
11. *(turn 0)* In REV, the power loss was less than in FWD.
12. *(turn 0)* In the P&T results, at 1 hr 6 min the central temperature was 85°C and the power loss was 2.8 kW.
13. *(turn 0)* In the P&T results, at 2 hours the central temperature was about 110°C and the power loss was 5.3 kW.
14. *(turn 0)* In the lube results, at horizontal position there were no signs of lubrication problems and the oil levels in inclined areas were not performed due to not suitable bench availability.
15. *(turn 0)* The final tear down was done after the following Hi-speed test 97_20.
16. *(turn 0)* The part partially passed the P&T + Lube test because it was improved but had the same power loss step.
17. *(turn 0)* In the P&T test, the temperature in the central remained below 120°C (max allowable) within the target 2 hours.
18. *(turn 0)* In the P&T test, the power loss step-up became irregular after 1 hr 6 min with no stabilization.
19. *(turn 0)* The power loss values were significantly lower than the reference test with the M2 axle and also lower than the 740 axle reference test 05_17.
20. *(turn 0)* In the lubrication assessment, the oil level was always above the minimum oil level in all compartments.
21. *(turn 0)* I was advised to not reduce the oil level further unless the power loss step-up is solved.
22. *(turn 0)* There was no oil leak and no lubrication problem.
23. *(turn 0)* The test start date was April 7th, 2021.
24. *(turn 0)* The test end date was April 8th, 2021.
25. *(turn 0)* The test engineer responsible was Eng. D. Zaglia.
26. *(turn 0)* The test location was Off Highway Arco, Italy.
27. *(turn 0)* The completion date was June 7th, 2021.
28. *(turn 0)* The requestor name was Mr. L. Fornoli.
29. *(turn 0)* The test materials included part name 755 with part number 10145957 and revision level Proto 0.
30. *(turn 0)* The model series was M2 21V CVT and the sample number was 1.
31. *(turn 0)* The axle serial number was C-ITA-374922 and it was Proto1.
32. *(turn 0)* The axle was assembled originally at Como and reassembled at Arco after tests 96_21 P&T and 97_21 Hi-speed.
33. *(turn 0)* The axle was installed at the test lab with transparent oil tubes for oil level monitoring on both arms, between pinion bearings, and between the pinion tail bearing and seal.
34. *(turn 0)* The axle total reduction ratio was 16.79.
35. *(turn 0)* The bevel set ratio was 14/37 LH Crown input pinion.
36. *(turn 0)* The hub reduction ratio i_hr was 6.353.
37. *(turn 0)* There were 4 planetary gears ground to standard production.
38. *(turn 0)* The arm seal part number was 10118272 (new Combi-Go).
39. *(turn 0)* The input seal part number was 760.04.012.01.
40. *(turn 0)* The sun gear cassette seal part number was 10152216.
41. *(turn 0)* The wheel hub cassette seal part number was 755.06.082.01.
42. *(turn 0)* The oil used was Total Tractelf SF3 (UTTO, DANA first fill).
43. *(turn 0)* The central oil quantity was 11.0 -0.5 lt. (below oil level).
44. *(turn 0)* The final drives oil quantity was 1.9 lt. each.
45. *(turn 0)* The test bench used transparent oil tubes for oil level monitoring and the operator was Giuseppe F.
46. *(turn 0)* The calibration data reference file was PRSSTS015011#001BF2.xlsx.
47. *(turn 0)* The test procedure used spinning at 53 km/h with horizontal position and FWD direction only.
48. *(turn 0)* The wheel radius was 0.73 m and the equivalent input rpm in was 3234 rpm.
49. *(turn 0)* The test had no torque/load and no steer.
50. *(turn 0)* The axle was in a horizontal position and there was no ventilation.
51. *(turn 0)* A temperature probe was placed on the central housing (oil drain plug).
52. *(turn 0)* The measured parameters were input speed, differential (drain oil plug) and ambient temperature.
53. *(turn 0)* The test cycle ramped speed from max (3234 rpm at pinion) to 0 rpm at ambient temperature 25°C, 60°C, and 90°C with tolerance ±5°C.
54. *(turn 0)* Air ventilation was used if necessary with cooling fans to adjust to the desired temperature.
55. *(turn 0)* The acceptance criterion for oil temperature was max 120°C oil according to std. DANA Reference TS.30.P&T.A.
56. *(turn 0)* The lubrication acceptance criterion was that oil level must not be lower than the lower roller axis so all bearings receive sufficient lubricant amount.
57. *(turn 0)* The acceptance criteria included no failure of any components and no leak.
58. *(turn 0)* The leak acceptance criterion referenced TS: max level 2 acceptable to SAE J1176.
59. *(turn 0)* The acceptance criteria included no sign of overheat or seizure on bearings.
60. *(turn 0)* At input speed 3234 rpm in FWD at 25°C, the central temperature was 8.1°C (with power loss 9.4 kW in parentheses).
61. *(turn 0)* At input speed 3234 rpm in FWD at 60°C, the central temperature was 110°C with a test time of 120 min and power loss 5.3 kW with a remark of 85°C / 2.8 kW at 60 minutes then power step-up without stabilization (25°C ambient).
62. *(turn 0)* At input speed 3234 rpm in FWD at 90°C, the central temperature was 3.6 kW (4.5 kW in parentheses) after run-in.
63. *(turn 0)* At input speed 3234 rpm in FWD at 60°C, the power loss was 5.2 kW (5.7 kW in parentheses).
64. *(turn 0)* The acquisition for ramp-up used 1 sample / 0.2" = 5 Hz and 1 hr = 18000 samples.
65. *(turn 0)* The test bench diagrams used a ramp-up acquisition scale of 5 Hz and 18000 samples per hour.
66. *(turn 0)* The P&T diagrams scale was referenced to real axle input speed with a bench in 2nd gear ratio of 1.61 and after post-process off-line graph.
67. *(turn 0)* The P vs rpm diagrams scale used axle input rpm multiplied by 1.36.
68. *(turn 0)* The test bench diagram file referenced 96_20 755-10145957 Proto1 M2 21V CVT MF with power loss 3234 rpm FWD and oil ELF SF3 at 0 hrs without ventilation after run-in.
69. *(turn 0)* The test bench diagram file referenced 40_21 755 MF M2-21 P&T 3234 rpm 120 min FWD with ambient target 120 min and oil ELF SF3 -0.5 lt in central housing.
70. *(turn 0)* The test bench diagram file referenced 40_21 755 MF M2-21 Pvs.rpm 53kph FWD -0.5lt at 25°C ambient with diff. lock 5 bar.
71. *(turn 0)* The test bench diagram file referenced 40_21 755 MF M2-21 Pvs.rpm 53kph FWD -0.5lt at T=90°C with diff. lock 5 bar.
72. *(turn 0)* The test bench diagram file referenced 40_21 755 MF M2-21 Pvs.rpm 53kph FWD -0.5lt at T=60°C with diff. lock 5 bar.
73. *(turn 0)* The test bench diagram file referenced 96_20 755-10145957 MF M2 21V CVT P&T 3234 rpm 120 min FWD with Tstart 30°C and oil type ELF SF3 and -85°C in 60 minutes marked OK.
74. *(turn 0)* The lube test included 10 kph and 53 kph speeds in FWD and REV at 20°C, 60°C, and 90°C.
75. *(turn 0)* The lube test included a 0° lube test at horizontal position.
76. *(turn 0)* The lube test included LH side (Diff.) and RH side (Crown) pinion side.
77. *(turn 0)* The lube test photos included 10 kph FWD at 20°C and 10 kph REV at 20°C.
78. *(turn 0)* The lube test photos included 53 kph REV at 20°C.
79. *(turn 0)* The lube test photos included 10 kph FWD at 60°C and 10 kph REV at 60°C.
80. *(turn 0)* The lube test photos included 53 kph FWD at 60°C.
81. *(turn 0)* The lube test photos included 10 kph FWD at 90°C (two variants labeled 90°C (1) and 90°C (2)) and 10 kph REV at 90°C.
82. *(turn 0)* The lube test photos included 53 kph FWD at 90°C.
83. *(turn 0)* The final tear down included a tear down of the central housing.
84. *(turn 0)* In the tear down, the pinion tail bearing cup was OK with some dentings probably due to disassembly.
85. *(turn 0)* In the tear down, the pinion back-head bearing cup was OK with dragged hard particles marks.
86. *(turn 0)* The test report referenced an appendix document titled “159_19 730-10134295 design validation.doc”.
87. *(turn 0)* The test request document path referenced “40_21 755-10145957 MF M2 P&T + Lube 2nd Test Request PT+Lube AGCO M2 21V CVT rev_01.docx”.
88. *(turn 0)* The test diary document referenced “40_21 755 MF M2 21V CVT P&T Lube test bench diagrams Rev.0.docs”.
89. *(turn 0)* The ISIR document referenced “ISIR 40_21 755 MF Foglio di allestimento GF Rev.0; Proto 1 P&TH.zip”.
90. *(turn 0)* The material analysis document referenced “Various DVPR_M2_21CVT_MF_06-03-20.xlsx”.

4. *(turn 0)* In post-hearing submissions, the builder cited Haller.

6. *(turn 0)* Leave to appeal is not required in respect of Ground 6.
7. *(turn 0)* Ground 6 concerns whether the Tribunal made an error of law in finding the completion date of works under the parties’ 2009 contract was 20 October 2010.
8. *(turn 0)* Ground 7 concerns whether leave to appeal should be granted because the Tribunal’s completion date finding was against the weight of evidence.
9. *(turn 0)* The owners argued that practical completion under the original contract took place on 20 October 2010 when the FOC issued.
10. *(turn 0)* The Tribunal agreed with the thrust of the owners' submission about practical completion taking place when the FOC issued.
11. *(turn 0)* The contract was a 2009 contract.
12. *(turn 0)* The completion date of the works under the 2009 contract was found to be 20 October 2010.
13. *(turn 2)* The contract was described as a standard form Housing Industry Association (HIA) contract.
14. *(turn 2)* Clause 1 of the contract defines “building works” as the building works to be carried out, completed and handed over to the owner in accordance with the contract as shown in the contract documents and including variations.
15. *(turn 2)* Clause 1 defines “practical completion” as when the building works are complete except for minor omissions and defects that do not prevent the building works from being reasonably capable of being used for their usual purpose.
16. *(turn 2)* Clause 7 of the contract is headed “Planning and Building Approvals”.
17. *(turn 2)* Clause 7.1 requires the party named in Item 11 of Schedule 1 to obtain and pay for all building and planning approvals.
18. *(turn 2)* Item 11 names the builder as the responsible party for the purposes of clause 7.
19. *(turn 2)* The builder was responsible for obtaining the FOC.
20. *(turn 2)* The builder’s obligation to provide the FOC fell within the definition of “building works” under the contract.
21. *(turn 2)* Completion of the works occurred within the meaning of the contract for the purposes of s 3B(1) of the HB Act upon the owners’ receipt of the Final Occupation Certificate (FOC).
22. *(turn 2)* The Tribunal’s completion-date finding was relevant to its finding that there was consideration in respect of the new contract it found the parties had entered into.
23. *(turn 2)* The Tribunal concluded that it erred in its interpretation of relevant terms of the contract.
24. *(turn 2)* The Tribunal’s reasoning process concluded that the builder’s obligation to provide the FOC was part of “building works” and that completion occurred upon the owners’ receipt of the FOC.
25. *(turn 2)* The Tribunal’s reasoning process concluded that the builder’s responsibility for obtaining the FOC supported the completion-date conclusion.
26. *(turn 2)* The Tribunal’s reasoning process concluded that the completion-date finding was relevant to consideration for the new contract.
27. *(turn 2)* The Tribunal’s reasoning process stated that it was not strictly necessary to consider Ground 6 and 7 given findings in respect of Grounds 2 and 3, but it did so for completeness.
28. *(turn 2)* The owners received the FOC as part of planning and building approvals under item 11 in Schedule 1.
29. *(turn 2)* The text states that a date is not required, just a specified activity, for the completion date under the contract interpretation.
30. *(turn 2)* The text references HBA s 3B(1), s 3B(2), and s 3B(3).

### `sharegpt_ipLglky_45`

- Facts: **23** · Keyphrases: **12** · Events: **2**

#### Facts

1. *(turn 1)* The contract clause 27 is headed “Final Certificate”.
2. *(turn 1)* Clause 27 comes after clause 26, which is headed “Practical Completion”.
3. *(turn 1)* Clause 27.1 states that, unless stated elsewhere in the contract, the builder is not required to obtain any certificate of occupancy or final inspection certificate relating to the building works.
4. *(turn 1)* Clause 29 is headed “Defects Liability Period”.
5. *(turn 1)* Clause 29.1 states that the defects liability period is 26 weeks commencing on and including the date of practical completion.
6. *(turn 1)* In clause 29.1, the standard form 13-week period was struck through and “26” was inserted and initialled.
7. *(turn 1)* Clause 29.2 states that the owner may, before the end of the defects liability period, give the builder a list of defects that appear after the date of practical completion.
8. *(turn 1)* Clause 29.3 states that the builder must rectify defects that are the builder’s responsibility and which are notified to the builder during the defects liability period.
9. *(turn 1)* Clause 14 of Schedule 1 specifies that Tender No 2277 dated 22 January 2009 forms part of the contract.
10. *(turn 1)* The Tender specifies that the basic tender price includes the Council Development Application and Inspection Fees.

- Facts: **29** · Keyphrases: **20** · Events: **6**

#### Facts

1. *(turn 0)* I do not accept that the definition of building works includes the rectification of defects in the building works notified during the defects liability period.

3. *(turn 0)* The contract defines practical completion as when building works are complete except for minor omissions and defects that do not prevent the building works from being reasonably capable of being used for their usual purpose.
4. *(turn 0)* The contract states that the defects liability period commences on and including the date of practical completion.
5. *(turn 0)* The contract states that before the end of the defects liability period the owner may give the builder one list of defects in the building works that appear after the date of practical completion.
6. *(turn 0)* Section 48K(7) stated that the Tribunal does not have jurisdiction in respect of a building claim arising from a breach of a statutory warranty implied under Part 2C if the date on which the claim was lodged is more than 7 years after the date the residential building work was completed.
7. *(turn 0)* Section 48K(7) also stated that if the work is not completed, the relevant date is the date for completion specified or determined in accordance with the contract, or if there is no such date, the date of the contract.
8. *(turn 0)* Section 3B was introduced by the Home Building Amendment Act 2011.
9. *(turn 0)* The amendments enacted by the Home Building Amendment Act 2011 were made for the purpose of the avoidance of doubt.
10. *(turn 0)* The amendments enacted by the Home Building Amendment Act 2011, including section 3B, extend to residential building work commenced or completed before 1 February 2012.
11. *(turn 0)* The contract in this case was entered into in 2009.
12. *(turn 0)* The completion of residential building work occurs on the date that the work is complete within the meaning of the contract under which the work was done.
13. *(turn 0)* If the contract does not provide for when work is complete (or there is no contract), the completion of residential building work occurs on practical completion of the work.
14. *(turn 0)* The completion of residential building work is practical completion when the work is completed except for any omissions or defects that do not prevent the work from being reasonably capable of being used for its intended purpose.
15. *(turn 2)* I took the position that practical completion of residential building work occurred on the earliest of the contractor handing over possession, the contractor last attending the site to carry out work (excluding defect remedy work not affecting practical completion), the issue of an occupation certificate authorising use or occupation, or (for owner-builder work) 18 months after the issue of the owner-builder permit.
16. *(turn 2)* I concluded that the statutory warranty period commenced on completion of the building works within the meaning of the contract, meaning when the works reached practical completion.
17. *(turn 2)* It is not in dispute that the owners took possession of the works on 13 July 2017.
18. *(turn 2)* I concluded that issue of the FOC is not the completion date of the works under section 3B.
19. *(turn 2)* I concluded that the Tribunal erred in finding that the date of completion of the building works was 20 October 2010 when the FOC was issued.
20. *(turn 2)* I concluded that the Tribunal misinterpreted the contract and did not correctly apply sections 48K(7) and 3B of the HB Act.
21. *(turn 2)* I concluded that ground 6 is established.
22. *(turn 2)* I concluded that if the completion date was not the date of practical completion (8 July 2010), it was the date on which the works were handed over to the owners (13 July 2021).
23. *(turn 2)* I concluded that the Tribunal’s finding concerning the completion date gives rise to an injustice to the builder which is reasonably clear.
24. *(turn 2)* I concluded that the parties did not enter into a new contract or vary their original contract.
25. *(turn 2)* I concluded that the respondent’s application to the Tribunal was out of time because the owners’ proceedings were not commenced within seven years of completion of the building works.
26. *(turn 2)* The Tribunal has no power to extend time for the making of an application under the HB Act.
27. *(turn 2)* I concluded that the appropriate orders are that the appeal be allowed, the decision under appeal be set aside, and the Tribunal proceedings be dismissed.
28. *(turn 2)* The Tribunal formed a provisional view that the builder should be ordered to pay the owners’ costs.
29. *(turn 2)* The case S & G Homes Pty Ltd t/as Pavilion Homes v Owen [2015] NSWCATAP 190 at [53] was cited regarding the Tribunal’s power to extend time.

#### Facts

1. *(turn 0)* I have a simulated dataset with n=1000 observations containing ACR, UAMCC, and DAH measure scores.
2. *(turn 0)* The unique healthcare organization identifier variable in the dataset is DCE_ID.
3. *(turn 0)* If a DCE has missing scores for all relevant quality measures, I want to assign a P4P score of missing.
4. *(turn 0)* I want to use the benchmark information from "Table 2-6. Hypothetical Benchmark Distributions for ACR and UAMCC for Comparison with Standard and New Entrant DCE Measure Scores".
5. *(turn 0)* In PY2022, ACR and UAMCC are P4P components.
6. *(turn 0)* In PY2022, the P4P component quality score is determined by the highest performance level (percentile) achieved for either ACR or UAMCC.
7. *(turn 0)* In PY2022, the P4P component is pass/fail such that DCEs with at least one measure at or exceeding the 30th percentile receive a 100% component quality score.
8. *(turn 0)* In PY2022, a sliding scale earn-back is applied for DCEs that do not meet the 30th percentile threshold on at least one of the two measures.
9. *(turn 0)* In PY2022, the sliding scale maps percentiles to P4P component quality scores: ≥30th to 100%, 25th to <30th to 95%, 20th to <25th to 80%, 15th to <20th to 60%, 10th to <15th to 40%, 5th to <10th to 20%, and <5th to 0%.
10. *(turn 0)* In PY2022, the P4R claims-based measures component quality score has a weight of 2/5 of the overall quality withhold.
11. *(turn 0)* In PY2022, the P4R CAHPS component quality score has a weight of 2/5 of the overall quality withhold.
12. *(turn 0)* In PY2022, the P4P component quality score has a weight of 1/5 of the overall quality withhold.
13. *(turn 0)* In PY2022, the Eligible Earn-Back Rate is 5% for all DCEs.
14. *(turn 0)* In PY2022, a DCE with a Total Quality Score of 96% has a Final Earn-Back Rate of 4.8%.
15. *(turn 0)* In PY2022, the Final Earn-Back Rate is determined during final reconciliation, which occurs in 2023.

#### Events

- Facts: **16** · Keyphrases: **11** · Events: **5**

#### Facts

1. *(turn 0)* I used Python with pandas, numpy, matplotlib, seaborn, and statsmodels to analyze a retweet dataset.
2. *(turn 0)* I loaded a CSV file named retweets_congress_115th.csv.gz using pandas.read_csv.
3. *(turn 0)* I excluded the full_text column when loading the dataset for speed.
4. *(turn 0)* I set dtype for tweet_id_str to str when reading the CSV.
5. *(turn 0)* I used index_col='tweet_id_str' when loading the dataset.
6. *(turn 0)* The loaded dataset had shape (209856, 21).
7. *(turn 0)* The dataset columns included user_id_str, user_screen_name, user_followers_count, created_at, retweet_count, retweeted_status, year, bioguide, birthday, gender, name, state, chamber, party, region, rt_user_id, rt_screen_name, rt_followers_count, rt_bio, age, and age_bucket.
8. *(turn 2)* I planned to compute the marginal probability of the party of the tweet and save it to party_marg.
9. *(turn 2)* I planned to create a new boolean column is_congress_retweet indicating whether the retweet is coming from a member of Congress.
10. *(turn 2)* I planned to get the set of unique user_id_str values using the .unique() method.
11. *(turn 2)* I planned to use the .isin(values) method on the rt_user_id column to create is_congress_retweet.
12. *(turn 2)* I planned to compute the marginal probability of retweeting a member of Congress and save it to congress_retweet_marg.
13. *(turn 2)* I planned to compute counts of retweets by each party and save them to party_cong_retweet_counts.
14. *(turn 2)* I planned to calculate and display the joint probability of party and whether the retweet comes from a member of Congress, saving it as party_cong_retweet_joint.
15. *(turn 2)* I planned to calculate the conditional probability of retweeting a member of Congress conditioning on the party.
16. *(turn 2)* I planned to compute expected joint probabilities under independence using np.outer and save them as party_cong_retweet_indep.

#### Keyphrases
